# marvin — Working Journal

Running log of planning, decisions, open questions, and work-in-progress for marvin. Newest entries at the top. For *what marvin is* (purpose, subsystems, interfaces, milestones), read [`spec.md`](spec.md) — this journal does not duplicate it.

---

**UI architecture — nav drawer complete; song/mode-select dialog is the next UI step.** The canvas compositor + per-screen-module model is built and validated on hardware: `ui_manager` orchestrates screens (string table + `screenInit/Show`; the MGS screen state machine is disabled, worked around with the `compat/le_gen_init.h` stub), the dashboard is its own MGS Screen on BASE, and the nav drawer is its own MGS Screen (`Navigation`) re-hosted onto OVR1 — they coexist live (no active-screen gate; `leUpdate`/`leInput` cover all attached layers). `LE_LAYER_COUNT`=3 is pinned via the never-shown `LayerBudget` screen. **Nav drawer is feature-complete:** slides in/out via canvas Move FX (`NAV_CLOSED_X` dodges the window-clip row-wrap; mid-slide reversal cancels the in-flight move), single-active highlight (Dashboard closes, others switch), clean close, rounded buttons with anti-aliased corners (`ui/widgets/button_aa/widget_button_aa.c`, per-instance vtable re-point — see `ui_compositor.md` §10a). Authoritative design + the window-clip/alignment gotchas in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md); decisions 2026-06-25. **Next (decided 2026-06-29): migrate to MGS's single-master-screen / layer-screen model** — one master Legato screen named `Marvin` with one layer per panel (dashboard, nav, song-select, dialogs…), replacing the current "separate 1-layer MGS Screen per panel, manually re-hosted, `LE_LAYER_COUNT` pinned by the fake `LayerBudget` screen" approach. MGS then derives `LE_LAYER_COUNT` and auto-associates each layer-screen with its canvas, so the `LayerBudget` hack, the `compat/le_gen_init.h` stub, and the disconnect/re-host dance all go away; `ui_manager` shrinks to buffer assignment + `show_layer`/`hide_layer` verbs. **Splash stays separate/manual** (pre-Legato RGBA8888) on a canvas slot outside the layer-screen range. Authoritative model + direction now in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §0. SongSelect (MGS screen already generated, committed `1e16ac9`) becomes a Marvin layer-screen rather than a standalone Screen. The earlier "parked surfaces aren't pickable / reveal-then-promote" framing is **superseded** (a state-machine artifact).

**Song-select operator UI — scrollable list widget built & validated on hardware.** Custom Legato widget `ui/song_list.{h,c}` (a hand-written `leWidget` subclass) renders the SELECT SONG setlist (tier badge + bold title + dim artist + right-aligned duration), driven by a decoupled row-provider callback (no catalog dependency); drag-scroll with time-based kinetic inertia, tap-to-select. Bring-up via the `songlist` console command + a catalog-backed demo provider (`ui/song_list_demo.{c,h}`) — scroll/select/opaque-fill confirmed on hardware. **Bringing it up surfaced and fixed a real Legato/GFX2D cache-coherency bug** (see decision log 2026-06-24): the CPU-rendered scratch buffer was cacheable, so the 2D-engine blit leaked stale pixels through opaque widgets — fixed by placing the scratch in non-cached DDR. **Next:** author the full SELECT SONG screen in MGS (album art + catalog-fed metadata panel + difficulty/mode/Select), and the **tier / career-setlist data model** (the `tier` badge needs the deferred catalog tier column). Two upstream Microchip Legato/gfx bugs to file (decision log).

**Song catalog loader — built and committed; exercised on hardware via the `catalog`/`songlist` console commands.** New `game/catalog.{h,c}` maps the recognizer's `(setlist, index)` → human labels (title/artist/bpm/length) from `<card>/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv`, so the UI/high-score display shows names instead of `#4`. Flat **CSV** (decided over the spec's JSON for the same reasons `results` is CSV — no on-device tokenizer, `FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1`, static buffers, pandas/Excel-editable). The two CSV helpers were extracted out of `results.c` into a shared `util/csv.{h,c}` and the catalog reuses them. Lazy-loads once into a fixed `[GP_N_SONGS]` static cache (file closed after load), so lookups are O(n) in RAM and don't fight `results.csv` for the single FatFs handle. `catalog` console command (`reload` / `ls` / `<main|bonus> <index>`) validates the path against a checked-in seed catalog (`data/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv`). Columns: `setlist,index,title,artist,album,bpm,length_s,year,genre,difficulty`. `title`/`artist`/`album` are real, sourced from the `SONGS` table in `tools/fetch_gh3_cover_art.py` (incl. proper UTF-8 — *Héroes del Silencio*, *Mauvais Garçon*); that script gained a `--catalog` mode that also fills `year`/`genre` from MusicBrainz on a networked run. `bpm`/`length_s`/`difficulty` are reserved-but-blank (GH3 surfaces no per-song difficulty; the authentic signal is the career tier, deferred). Catalog is labels only: a missing/stale card degrades to "Unknown song" and never touches recognition (compile-time in flash). **Deferred:** the stale-catalog version warning — `export_c.py` doesn't emit `GP_METADATA_VERSION` yet (open item below). **Next SD consumer:** album-artwork pre-cache (spec §4.8.7) — directory structure is now locked (`art/{small,large}/<setlist>-<NN>.{jpg,png}`, one size-tier subdir per DDR cache), marvin decode side not yet built.

**T1S inter-node link — built and working (marvin ↔ guitar).** marvin is the PLCA **coordinator** (node 0) on a 10BASE-T1S bus (LAN8651 over FLEXCOM4 SPI); hand-authored stack in `default/src/net/t1s/`. It sends the actuator command over T1S to the `guitar` follower node (id 2), which actuates the Wii GPIOs (guitar G1+G2 proven). Followers announce presence via a heartbeat (ethertype `0x88B6`); the `t1s` and `nodes` console commands report link/credits/per-node presence. Transport is behind `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT={UART,T1S}` (T1S default on the `t1s` branch). The `fretboard` node (id 1) firmware is now **written, T1S-only** (fretboard journal 2026-06-17): it streams its 17-byte frame to marvin **and** drives the `guitar` node directly — its on-device model infers the bitmask and sends it peer-to-peer to `02:..:02`. marvin's RX side needed no changes — `fretboard_link.c`'s T1S frame handler already parses the frame from node id 1 (`DETECTOR_ADC_FRETBOARD`) and the node-table + heartbeat-presence demux already cover it. **Next (marvin side):** **active-source arbitration** — both marvin (timing pipeline) and the fretboard now address the guitar (`02:..:02`), so marvin needs active-detector/active-guitar selection to ensure exactly one source drives at a time (the fretboard's SW0 is an interim manual arm; until then, don't run marvin's timing pipeline to the guitar while the fretboard is armed). Then fretboard hardware bring-up. PoDL is design-direction only. Detail in [`../../../docs/t1s-podl-link.md`](../../../docs/t1s-podl-link.md); decision log entry 2026-06-17.

**Board port: SAM9X75 Curiosity → Curiosity Hybrid (in progress).** USB Host is gone on the Hybrid; the fretboard link was rewired from USB CDC host to a direct FLEXCOM2 USART on 2026-06-08 (see decision log + session log). Code-complete and syntax-checked; pending a full MPLAB build and on-hardware bring-up. Display/MIPI I²C (incl. TC358743 control) also moved off FLEXCOM6/PA24-PA25 to FLEXCOM8 TWI/PB4-PB5 during the port — `spec.md` §3.1/§3.2 reconciled on 2026-06-08. Dated 2026-05-01/05-20 journal entries and `journal-archive.md` keep their FLEXCOM6 references as historical record (true on the original Curiosity board).

**M1 — Reference detector v0.** First CV detector running on captured frames, publishing `detector_state_t` records onto the detector-state bus. No actuation yet. See [`spec.md`](spec.md) §4.2 for subsystem detail and §8 for the milestone progression.

Scaffold is in place as of 2026-05-20: `detector/detector.{h,c}` owns the bus queue + a temporary drain task that logs records-per-second; `detector/cv_marvin_v1.{h,c}` subscribes to the video frame queue and publishes one `detector_state_t` per frame with `frame_epoch = Video_FrameInfo.frame_count` and all-zero fret state. Next: pick the v0 algorithm (Q8) and start populating `fret[]`.

**Capture/display pipeline is done as of 2026-05-02:** source → TC358743 → CSI-2 @ 972 Mbps/lane → SAM9X75 → ISC DMA → DDR (BGRX32) → XLCDC OVR1 → LVDSC → 10.1″ 1280×800 LVDS panel.

> **Deeper-dive references:**
> - [`spec.md`](spec.md) — system-level spec (read first).
> - [`capture_pipeline.md`](capture_pipeline.md) — HDMI → DDR capture stage (CSI2DC, ISC, register settings, datasheet citations).
> - [`display_path.md`](display_path.md) — DDR → LCD display stage (XLCDC OVR1 wiring, pillarbox, cache coherence).

> **Earlier entries** (capture/display bring-up, 2026-05-01 → 2026-05-15) are preserved in [`journal-archive.md`](journal-archive.md).

## Decision log

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|------|----------|-----------|
| 2026-06-29 | **Migrate the operator UI to MGS's single-master-screen / layer-screen model: one master Legato screen `Marvin` whose *layers* are the panels (dashboard, nav, song-select, dialogs…), replacing the current "separate 1-layer MGS Screen per panel, manually re-hosted by `ui_manager`, `LE_LAYER_COUNT` pinned by a fake `LayerBudget` screen."** Per Microchip's GFX-canvas **layer-screen** guide, this is the framework's intended structure: MGS derives `LE_LAYER_COUNT` from the layer count and auto-associates each layer-screen with its own GFX canvas, so the `LayerBudget` pin, the `compat/le_gen_init.h` stub, and the `init_screens()` build-on-layer-0-then-disconnect + per-screen `*_OnShow` re-host dance all disappear. `ui_manager` shrinks to: assign each layer-screen's canvas buffer (content-sized, RGB565) + `show_layer(idx, hwLayer)` / `hide_layer(idx)` verbs (HW-layer binding stays a runtime `gfxcSetLayer` choice, never owned by a panel). Panel modules (`screen_nav.c`, `screen_song_select.c`, …) keep content + event wiring + interaction FX but drop all `leAddRootWidget`/canvas mechanics. **Splash stays separate/manual** (pre-Legato RGBA8888 scanout) on a canvas slot *outside* the layer-screen range 0..N-1 (e.g. top of the 8-slot pool). The three-concept model that drove this (Legato layer-screen ↔ canvas coupled 1:1; XLCDC HW layer independent/runtime) + the direction are now canonical in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §0. Open items before coding: final layer order/z-order + initial layer set; per-layer-screen canvas sizing/offset; MGS widget naming across layers (drives a rename sweep in the panel modules); confirm a hidden layer-screen's canvas suppresses touch picking. | The current approach hand-emulates layer-screens, which is what forced every recurring canvas-vs-layer confusion: with `LE_LAYER_COUNT`=3 only canvases 0/1/2 are real Legato render targets, so a 3rd live panel could not get its own slot without colliding with the splash's. Adopting the framework model makes "each panel owns its own canvas" literally true (define up to 8 layer-screens, composite any 3 onto the HW layers), aligns with the tooling instead of fighting it, deletes two standing hacks, and is purpose-built for marvin's need (multiple coexisting overlays with preserved widget state, no redraw on transition). Splash stays out of the master screen because it must paint before Legato/the master screen is built (fast-boot path). |  (`data/ui/make-splash.sh`), not by ad-hoc shell history; the raw byte order is `[A,B,G,R]` per pixel.** The XLCDC splash canvas is `GFX_COLOR_MODE_RGBA_8888` whose pixel word is `0xRRGGBBAA` (`splash.c` `SPLASH_FILL 0x000000FF` = opaque black confirms it), so little-endian memory bytes are `[A,B,G,R]` — **not** Pillow's default `R,G,B,A`. The script splits channels and `Image.merge("RGBA",(a,b,g,r))` to get that order, resizes to 1280×800, and asserts the 4,096,000-byte size `splash.c` requires. Uses `uv` + Pillow (no ImageMagick/ffmpeg — neither is installed on this host). Verified empirically against the committed `splash.raw`: first pixel matched to within JPEG-re-encode noise, while the naive `R,G,B,A` ordering was fully reversed. Regenerated `splash.raw` from the (newer) `splash.jpg`, which had drifted out of sync. Documented in `data/README.md`; flash with `openocd/program-qspi.sh splash`. | The conversion step previously lived only in someone's shell history — undocumented and reproducible only by guessing the byte order (easy to get reversed, since the framework name "RGBA_8888" reads like Pillow's `R,G,B,A` but the little-endian word layout inverts it). A checked-in script with the size assertion makes the splash pipeline self-serve and drift-proof, and pairs with the existing `program-qspi.sh splash` provisioning step. |
| 2026-06-28 | **QSPI 8 MiB partition map locked — `flash/qspi_layout.h` is the single source of truth.** Splash blob `0x000000` (4 MiB) · UI assets `0x400000` (~3.98 MiB) · Settings ring `0x7FC000` (16 KiB / 4 sectors). All boundaries 4 KiB sector-aligned; contiguous, no gaps/overlaps. Splash at offset 0 (raw pre-decoded framebuffer; 4 MiB holds full-panel RGBA8888 = 3.91 MiB, or an RGB565 splash uses ~1.95 MiB — the reservation keeps both open, format decided when the loader is built). Read-mostly bulk grows upward; the small, frequently-erased settings ring sits at the very top as a stable anchor, **wear-isolated** from the bulk. Settings get 4 sectors (2× the 2-sector ping-pong minimum) for ring headroom. UI assets indexed by **compile-time offsets** for now (flashed with firmware); a manifest sector / littlefs can be carved from the front of the assets region later if assets must update independently. | One header (`qspi_layout.h`) means consumers and the `qspi` dev tools share offsets — no drift. Settings-at-top isolates erase wear and gives a fixed anchor independent of how the bulk grows. **Dev-tool caveat:** `qspi` smoke (last sector `0x7FF000`) and `qspi verify` (top-down) write into the settings region — destructive to it, but the ring-log's CRC-fallback-to-defaults makes that non-fatal (settings just reinitialize). Run heavy `qspi verify` before the settings store is populated, or accept a settings reset. | 
| 2026-06-28 | **QSPI read throughput — the serial clock (GCLK = 2×QSCK) was the lever, not per-access overhead. At 200 MHz GCLK (MCC max → 100 MHz QSCK), XDMAC mem2mem reads hit 18.2 MB/s → ~225 ms for a 4 MB splash, ~3× faster than SD (~750 ms). Read integrity verified at volume. Splash → QSPI via XDMAC mem2mem is GO.** Bench (`qspi bench`, `flash/qspi_smoke.c`) at 50 MHz GCLK → 25 MHz QSCK showed ~5 MB/s and I wrongly concluded "overhead-bound, clock maxed." Greg caught the datasheet note **GCLK must = 2×QSCK** and raised GCLK 50→200 MHz; everything scaled ~linearly (DRV_SST26_Read 4.6→14.8, XDMAC mem2mem 4.9→18.2 MB/s; the raw libc `memcpy` path stays ~2.5 — it is just bad on this region, ignore it). Read path = **XDMAC channel 1 mem2mem from `QSPIMEM_ADDR` (0x60000000)** — ch0 is the ISC histogram DMA, and the PLIB only tracks ch0, so we program ch1's registers directly. Integrity confirmed by `qspi verify 1024 4`: 1 MiB × 4 passes via **both** the driver and XDMAC, **0 mismatches** at 100 MHz, with a position-dependent pattern (catches misaddressing, not just stuck bits). **Two dead ends ruled out:** (a) making the QSPI window **cacheable** in the MMU **hangs** — cache-linefill bursts aren't serviced by the SMM slave (back out; XDMAC bypasses the MMU so it's unaffected); (b) **continuous-read mode (CRM)** only trims the instruction byte on *non-sequential* access, so it does nothing for bulk sequential reads. SMM tops out ~36% of the 50 MB/s theoretical (re-frames per access); the only path to ~50 MB/s (~80 ms) is the QSPI's RX-triggered streaming read — deferred as a mini-project, not worth it over 225 ms. | The clock fix flipped the verdict: at 25 MHz QSCK QSPI-SMM (~5 MB/s, ~800 ms) was *worse* than SD's read; at 100 MHz QSCK (18.2 MB/s) it clearly wins. 100 MHz is within the SST26's 104 MHz rating and proved data-clean at volume, so no DLL/read-training needed at this rate. 200 MHz GCLK is an MCC clock-config change (lives in MCC config → survives regen). **Gotcha for any raw SMM read (memcpy/DMA, bypassing `DRV_SST26_Read`):** the read frame must be closed with `QSPI_CR.LASTXFER` + wait-CSRA or the next QSPI command hangs with CS still asserted — the driver does this internally; raw paths must call an equivalent (`qspi_end_smm_read()` in the bench). Splash framebuffer is in `.region_nocache` (strongly-ordered 0x20000000) but XDMAC writes bypass the MMU, so DMA→framebuffer is unaffected. | 
| 2026-06-28 | **Config/operator settings (backlight %, mode toggles, calibration) persist to the onboard QSPI NOR (`SST26VF064BA`, 8 MB / 64 Mbit SQI) via flash EEPROM-emulation — a small wear-leveled ring-log, NOT a filesystem. No board EEPROM exists, so QSPI is the only non-volatile small-state store.** Reserve ≥2× 4 KB sectors as a ping-pong region: a save appends a `{seq, settings_t, CRC}` record to the next free slot in the active sector (page-program, *no erase*); when full, write the latest record into the pre-erased partner sector then erase the full one. Boot picks the highest-`seq` record with a valid CRC; a partial trailing record (power lost mid-write) fails CRC and falls back to the prior good record → **power-fail safe by construction**, no torn settings. This supersedes the spec §4.6.2/§4.7 plan to keep all config in `/marvin/config.json` on the SD card: operator/UI prefs must persist and load **even with no SD card inserted** (the SD is removable, QSPI is always present). Session/recording config that travels with the data can stay on SD. **Splash, when/if it moves to QSPI, is stored as a raw pre-decoded framebuffer blob at a fixed offset — also no filesystem.** | NOR semantics drive the design: bits only clear 1→0 on program; setting a bit back to 1 requires erasing the whole 4 KB sector, so "update a value" = erase+rewrite 4 KB. FAT makes that worse (every file write also rewrites FAT/dir sectors → write amplification) and FatFs is *not* power-loss-atomic → a mid-write power cut can corrupt the FAT itself, not just the value. The ring-log avoids both: erases are rare (~one per N saves, N = slots/sector) and alternate across sectors, giving effectively unlimited endurance for human-driven settings against the part's ~100k cycles/sector; atomicity is the seq+CRC scan. littlefs (Harmony File System can back it) is the fallback **only** if settings grow into many named, workstation-mountable keys — overkill for a handful of scalars. **Raw-only splash:** the seed `splash.jpg` carries ~2 s of on-target JPEG decode, which dwarfs any bringup savings, so a JPEG-on-QSPI splash would be *slower* than today's SD path — only a raw blob streamed straight to the framebuffer (no decode, no FS mount, no FTL — NOR has none) realizes QSPI's sub-ms bringup vs. SD's ~350 ms `ACMD41` power-up handshake. | 
| 2026-06-26 | **Boot splash = full-screen SD JPEG shown on BASE before the backlight comes on; first real use of the compositor "replace" verb. Code-complete, pending hardware test.** The `Splash` MGS screen (`le_gen_screen_Splash.c`: full-screen solid-fill `Splash_Panel_0`) is the base — kept for the "every base view has an MGS component" convention; `ui/screens/splash/screen_splash.c` adds one `leImageWidget` onto that panel in code (the photo is a runtime SD asset, can't be an MGS asset). A new boot loader task (`ui/loader.c`) mounts the card, reads `/<card>/ui/splash.jpg` into a static buffer, builds a runtime `leImage` (format JPEG) + sets it on the widget, then `UiManager_EnableBacklight()` (backlight control moved out of `video` into `ui_manager` — the UI owns the display; it was only in `video` for historical reasons), and reveals the dashboard + brings video up. **Per-screen pre-rendered persistent canvases** (revised within-session — see bring-up #1/#2): every screen is MGS-**persistent** (built once, never torn down) and owns its own canvas/Legato-layer/HW-layer — Dashboard 0/0/BASE (RGB565), Nav 1/1/OVR1 (RGB565), Splash 2/2/OVR2 (RGBA8888, full-screen, topmost). The splash is drawn over the dashboard on OVR2; the heavy Dashboard paints **behind** it and is revealed (hide OVR2) already complete. **Off-screen paint:** all screens are built pre-scheduler (no concurrency); the normal Legato render task paints them once the scheduler is up, and the loader simply pends on the public `leRenderer_IsIdle()` before lighting the panel (no scheduler hacking — see bring-up #4/#5 for the suspend/pump dead-end). RGB888 splash via the canvas's own 32bpp mode (the canvas framework sets the HW-layer color mode from `pixelBuffer.mode`); the RGB565↔8888 BASE-swap of the first cut is gone. Missing/oversized/no-card → the panel's solid fill is the fallback. Seed `data/ui/splash.jpg` (placeholder). Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §12–§13. | "Turn on the display" = enable the backlight (XLCDC scans continuously from `SYS_Initialize`), so gating the backlight on splash-ready is the whole mechanism. First on-hardware run showed reveal-before-paint noise (screens shown mid-paint over uninitialized DRAM); the fix is to paint each screen to completion off-screen, then reveal — the §1 compositor ideal, enabled by persistent screens + pumping the renderer to idle off-screen. JPEG over BMP/RAW: BMP isn't Legato-decodable (only RAW/JPEG/PNG), so it would mean a custom parser + bypassing the `leImageWidget` path and would prove nothing reusable; JPEG reuses Legato's enabled decoder and **is** the album-art pre-load path. RGB888-without-hoops confirmed in `gfx_canvas.c` (per-canvas mode auto-configures the XLCDC layer), so the splash gets full color depth while the steady-state dashboard stays RGB565 to save DDR bandwidth; the 32bpp splash buffer doubles as future album-art decode scratch. Splash kept as an MGS shell (Greg's call) for base-view uniformity even though its only real content is the loaded image; the image widget is code-added since it's runtime data. Loader is a task (not `APP_Initialize`) because SD mount + FatFs + decode block on I/O and need the scheduler. Known one-time-boot roughness (hard splash→dashboard cut; fixed backlight settle vs a real paint-complete signal; baseline-JPEG-only) tracked in §10/§12. | 
| 2026-06-26 | **Anti-aliased rounded button corners via a per-instance vtable re-point — no Legato/MCC edits (`ui/widgets/button_aa/widget_button_aa.c`).** The classic skin draws rounded corners with `leRenderer_ArcFill` (hard stepped edges). `ButtonAA_Enable(btn)` makes one shared writable copy of the button vtable, overrides only `_paint`, and points the button's `fn`/`widget.fn` at the copy; the wrapper runs the original paint then, on `DONE` at the supported radius, overdraws the four corners — sampling each corner's extreme (untouched) pixel as backdrop and blending a 1px band from a precomputed coverage mask (`leColorLerp` + `leRenderer_PutPixel`). Wired from `nav_buttons_init()` (nav-only for now; extend by adding call sites). Also added a CLAUDE.md rule: do not modify MCC-generated/Legato code under `config/default/`. Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §10a. | Editing the skin (the obvious spot) is forbidden — it's vendor/MCC code that regen silently overwrites. `LE_DYNAMIC_VTABLES` (the sanctioned global override) is **not** an MCC knob and is an unguarded `#define 0` in generated `legato_config.h` (can't flip via config, can't `-D`, can't hand-edit) — dead end. A post-render overdraw on the nav canvas has no vendor-legal post-paint hook and fights Legato's damage-repaint (1-frame hard-corner flash). The per-instance re-point exploits that `leWidget.fn` is a *writable* pointer to a const vtable: it needs no config flag, costs one vtable struct + one mask in BSS (static-allocation discipline holds), runs **in-pass** (repaint→AA same paint, no timing window), and is scoped to exactly the buttons we re-point. **Confirmed on hardware** — smooth corners across selected/unselected/pressed states and slide cycles. |
| 2026-06-25 | **Re-enabled GFX Canvas Move FX to animate the nav drawer slide (reverses the earlier "FX off, do our own" call).** The nav drawer now slides in/out by animating its OVR1 canvas window position via `gfxcStartEffectMove` (DEC ease-out), rather than snapping. With a concrete slide need this is the textbook canvas-move case (the doc flagged it as where canvas FX earns its weight); rolling our own would just duplicate the FX timer + `GFX_CANVAS_Task` we already have, and the FX timer only ticks during an active move (no steady cost). Open slides −off→0; entry taps switch the highlight (Dashboard also closes); a move-done callback (`nav_fx_done`) disables OVR1 when a close finishes. **Window-clip gotcha (cost a few iterations):** a canvas layer can't sit truly off-screen — the clip emulates negative X by shifting the source pointer + shrinking the window, but it first aligns X *down* to a multiple of 4 (`x &= ~0x3`, non-32bpp) and wraps a row once \|X\| hits the surface width, briefly flashing the panel's left columns; and the layer update busy-waits for the vsync latch, so that bad frame displays before the hide. Fix: slide to the furthest 4-aligned X with \|X\| < NAV_W (`NAV_CLOSED_X`, derived from NAV_W — not a magic number) so the final shown frame is an in-bounds edge sliver, then `nav_fx_done` disables the layer to clear it. | Canvas Move FX over a hand-rolled stepper because the machinery already exists (just was disabled) and it's purpose-built for sliding a layer window with easing; the earlier FX-off decision was speculative (pre-slide). The off-screen constant is derived + documented (points at the exact clip line) rather than hard-coded, accepting a documented coupling to the generated canvas clip — same class as the cache/scratch and HEO-blender couplings. Full decoupling (own the slide via direct XLCDC position stepping) is the fallback if the clip bites again. |
| 2026-06-25 | **UI architecture: disable the MGS screen state machine and let the app own screen orchestration, so panels coexist as live, independently-interactive LCDC layers — `ui_manager` + per-screen modules. Authoritative design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md).** Confirmed in the Legato core that `_state.layerList` is global, `leUpdate` renders/updates **every** attached layer each frame, and `leInput` picks touch across **all** attached layers top-to-bottom — there is no "active screen" render/pick gate. The only thing enforcing one-screen-at-a-time was `le_gen_init.c`'s state machine (`legato_showScreen` tears down the outgoing tree). Turning it off (and working around the MGS dangling-`le_gen_init.h` bug with a `compat/` stub) means we attach multiple panels' roots to different layers and they're all live + interactive at once. **Model:** each panel is authored as its own MGS Screen used as a *widget-tree factory* (`screenInit_` builds, `screenShow_` attaches + raises its `OnShow` composition root); `leAddRootWidget`/`leRemoveRootWidget` re-host a root onto whatever layer we choose. Layers: dashboard=BASE/0, nav=OVR1/1 (resident → "same nav over every view" free), modal song/mode dialog=OVR2/2 (sized to the dialog so live dashboard telemetry shows around it); camera=HEO outside Legato. Two verbs — **coexist** (overlay-as-layer, background live) and **replace** (swap base view on layer 0) — chosen per interaction, both owned by `ui_manager`, so the choice is reversible. `compositor.c` is promoted to `ui_manager`; per-screen/overlay modules own only their widgets+wiring. **This supersedes the earlier "parked surfaces aren't pickable / reveal-then-promote" idea in the doc** — that was a symptom of the state machine, now removed. | Greg's call to author nav + dialogs as separate MGS Screens (cleaner than overlapping layers in one Screen) collided with Legato's one-active-screen model; the resolution was to drop that model entirely rather than fight it, which the global-layerList facts make safe. Disabling the state machine also removes MGS's per-screen switch baggage outright (deleted code, not dormant) and hands us `screenInit/Show` directly. Live-coexistence (vs. freeze) was required because some dashboard telemetry must keep updating under the nav/dialog (the camera already self-updates on HEO). Per-screen modules + a `ui_manager` orchestrator keep each panel's wiring local (composition-root-per-screen) while one top-level module owns the scarce layer budget — matching the MCC-isolation idiom (own files via `user.cmake`). 3 UI layers + camera is the hard ceiling. | 
| 2026-06-25 | **The GFX Canvas regen pushed FreeRTOS `heap_1` past 40 KB → silent boot hang (zero DBGU output); raised `configTOTAL_HEAP_SIZE` 40960 → 100000.** Symptom: after the canvas MCC commit (`ed54f37`), marvin produced no DBGU output at all — bisected to that commit (`8de7c64` working). Not the compositor (it was stashed), and not the XLCDC OVR setup (BASE/HEO use the identical `LCDC_ATTRE`/`WAIT_ATTRS_EQ(SIP)` pre-`Start` pattern that already works, so OVR is symmetric). Root cause: the canvas additions (the dynamic `GFX_CANVAS_Task` + extra OSAL/driver allocations on the 4-layer/canvas display path) tipped cumulative `pvPortMalloc` during `SYS_Initialize` past the 40 KB `heap_1`. Because `configUSE_MALLOC_FAILED_HOOK=1` and `vApplicationMallocFailedHook()` is the MCC default (`taskDISABLE_INTERRUPTS(); for(;;){}`), the failed alloc spun with IRQs off **before** `APP_Initialize`/`log_init` (initialization.c:499) ran → no banner, indistinguishable from a dead board. **Two durable follow-ups (both carried):** (1) the heap size is **not** in `FreeRTOS.yml` — it's a raw literal in the generated `FreeRTOSConfig.h`, so the bump is an **MCC re-apply patch** (joins patch #8, also a `FreeRTOSConfig.h` edit) and will silently revert on the next regen → hang returns; set it in the MCC FreeRTOS component if it exposes Total Heap Size, else keep it on the re-apply list. (2) Patch `vApplicationMallocFailedHook` **and** `vApplicationStackOverflowHook` to emit a marker before the spin — a one-line DBGU write would have turned this multi-step bisect into an instant diagnosis. | Same silent-spin footgun already flagged for `vApplicationStackOverflowHook` (Open questions), now confirmed to bite via the malloc path too — heap exhaustion and stack overflow both present as identical dead-board hangs with the stock hooks. `heap_1` (never frees) is correct for marvin's static-allocation discipline, but MCC tasks + Legato OSAL still need it, so its size is load-bearing and must be tracked. 100000 is generous (+60 KB) headroom for now; right-size later once boot logs `xPortGetFreeHeapSize()`. Found by Greg's heap hypothesis after the XLCDC poll-loop theory was ruled out by BASE/HEO symmetry. |
| 2026-06-25 | **`GFX_CANVAS_Task` runs at priority 2 (the UI band), set via the gfx_canvas component's Task Priority field — not the generated default of 1.** Band 2 is marvin's UI tier (`LEGATO`, `DRV_MAXTOUCH`, `XLCDC`, `SYS_INPUT`; see 2026-05-21 priority re-tiering); the canvas task joins it as a fifth member. Stack 1024 / 10 ms delay kept (matches the other MCC UI tasks; FX is off so it mostly idles between updates). | The canvas task commits Legato's rendered output to the hardware layers (`gfxcShowCanvas`/`CanvasUpdate`/bind) — it *is* a UI task, peer to `XLCDC_Tasks` and downstream of `LEGATO_Tasks`, so it belongs in the same band under the established rule "a late UI paint is jitter only → UI stays the lowest *active* band and yields to vision (4) and strum-critical (5)." The generated default of **1** is wrong twice over: band 1 is **reserved** (future housekeeping/watchdog), and it would sit an active critical-path task below its UI peers. | 2026-06-25 | **Operator-UI presentation layer = a marvin compositor over the GFX Canvas component: pre-render UI panels into static non-cached RAM surfaces, multiplex the two free LCDC overlay layers (`OVR1`/`OVR2`) across them. Design captured in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md).** The goal is to bring a panel up (or slide it in) instantly without re-rendering or redrawing what's behind it. Confirmed the enabling fact in code: Legato's only "where do I render" knob is the active layer's `pixelBuffer.pixels` (it paints a scratch buffer, then `DRV_XLCDC_BlitBuffer` copies into `drvLayer[activeLayer].pixelBuffer`); there is **no** stock Legato API to render a widget tree into an arbitrary off-screen buffer. **GFX Canvas is exactly that missing indirection** — `GFXC_GetPixelBuffer` hands Legato a canvas object's buffer and `gfxcSetBaseCanvasID` switches which canvas a layer renders into, with `gfxcSetLayer`/show/hide/position/alpha + fade/move on top. Layer budget: `BASE`=background, `OVR1`/`OVR2`=swappable overlays (to be enabled — `XLCDC_TOT_LAYERS` is 2 today), `HEO`=live camera (off-limits). Legato stays the **renderer** (fonts, `song_list`); a thin `ui/` compositor owns surface↔layer multiplexing. Touch handled by **reveal-then-promote** (parked surfaces are non-interactive; promote to a live Legato layer to interact). Phased plan P0–P5 + open questions in the doc. **Design only — no code/MCC yet.** | Reverses the earlier "back off canvas" — that call was made on the **wrong axis**: canvas was dismissed for the *slide animation* (which direct `XLCDC_SetLayerWindowXYPos` stepping handles fine, no framework needed), but the actual problem is the **off-screen surface pool** (render content decoupled from a hardware layer), and that is canvas's literal reason to exist. The two homegrown alternatives lose: DIY pixel-buffer retarget reimplements canvas's per-surface/scratch/binding bookkeeping (~half of canvas, growing once surfaces aren't full-screen); direct-layer-only can present/animate but can't get Legato content *into* a surface. Canvas's earlier objections are addressable, not fatal: it deletes the static `frame_buffer` array, but we *use* the canvas buffers as our surfaces (statically allocated, non-cached per the 2026-06-24 fix), so static-allocation holds; the dynamic `GFX_CANVAS_Task` converts to `xTaskCreateStatic` or rides alongside the existing MCC dynamic tasks. Binding constraint is **layers (2 free), not memory** (~21 MB free in the 32 MB `ram_nocache` after the ISC pool + scratch; full-screen RGB565 ≈ 2 MB, most overlays far less). Resolves spec Q5 for the *presentation* layer (Legato renderer + canvas compositor) without dictating per-screen authoring. |
| 2026-06-24 | **Legato CPU-render scratch buffer must live in non-cached DDR (`.region_nocache`) — fixed a GFX2D cache-coherency bleed; grew `ram_nocache` 16→32 MB.** Symptom (found during song-list bring-up): dashboard content streakily "bled through" an opaque overlay widget, intermittently, only where CPU-rendered text was. Active render path is **CPU software** (`leRenderer_FillArea` → `_RGBBlendFill`; glyphs too) into a **scratch buffer**, then **`DRV_GFX2D_Blit`** (the SAM9X75 2D engine) copies scratch→framebuffer. The framebuffer is non-cached (`FB_CACHE_NC` → `.region_nocache`) but the Legato scratch was **cacheable**: `LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR` was an empty no-op `#define` and `LE_COHERENT_ATTR = CACHE_ALIGN` (alignment only). The blit's `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr` of the cached scratch is insufficient on ARM926 → the 2D engine reads stale CPU-cached lines → streaky, cache-line-granularity bleed. **Fix:** define `LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR = __attribute__((section(".region_nocache")))` in **`user.cmake`** (the `#ifndef` guard in the generated `legato_renderer.c` lets the `-D` win → survives MGS regen, no generated-tree patch); MCC grew `ram_nocache` 16→32 MB to host it (ddram.ld + plib_mmu.c + `DDRAM_NO_CACHE_SIZE`). **Confirmed on hardware** (commits `d36640f` + `c0f316c`). **Two upstream Microchip Legato/gfx bugs to file:** (1) with "Cacheable Frame Buffers" **off**, the generator non-caches the framebuffer but leaves the Legato render scratch cacheable (`LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR` empty) — the gap that bit us; (2) `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` omits the `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr` that `DRV_GFX2D_Blit` has (latent — bites any GPU-fill config, e.g. `LE_WIDGET_BUFFER_ENABLE=1`). | Root-caused by elimination, not by reading (every software-logic path traced correct): a bare opaque magenta fill still bled, hiding the dashboard cleared it, and non-cached scratch fixed it → it's the cached-scratch ↔ 2D-engine coherency, below the software layer. Non-cached is the correct policy on this SoC — Microchip already chose it for the framebuffer (`FB_CACHE_NC`) rather than trusting cache maintenance for a DMA-/GPU-read buffer; the scratch is the same kind of buffer and should match. Adding cache maintenance to `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` would *not* fix this (Fill isn't on the path; the on-path `Blit` already invalidates and still leaked). System-wide rule for this board: any buffer the 2D engine or display DMA reads must be non-cached (or carry correct, sufficient maintenance — which the cacheable path here does not). |
| 2026-06-24 | **Song-select setlist = a hand-written custom Legato widget (`leWidget` subclass), not the stock list widget.** `ui/song_list.{h,c}`: subclass with overridden `_paint`/`update`/touch handlers. Stock `leListWidget` is disabled **and** its item model (icon + one string) can't render the tier-badge + two-line + right-aligned-duration row. Design: **row-provider callback** `songlist_row_fn` (widget never includes `game/catalog.h`; the screen owns catalog→row mapping + future tier grouping); **drag + kinetic inertia** computed from the **FreeRTOS tick** (`leUpdate(0)` means the framework `dt` is always 0, so the widget measures real elapsed time itself for frame-rate-independent motion); **tap-to-select** (<10 px movement) with a selection callback; **virtualized software paint** (`leRenderer_RectFill` + `leStringRenderer_DrawCString`, visible rows only, no per-row child widgets); single **pool allocation** with the vtable built by **copying the base vtable at runtime** (project builds `LE_DYNAMIC_VTABLES=0`, so `_leWidget_FillVTable` isn't linkable and the GenerateVTable path is compiled out); declared **opaque** (`BACKGROUND_FILL`) so Legato composites it. Lives entirely in `ui/` (added via `user.cmake`) — zero edits to the generated `gfx/legato/` tree. Bring-up: `ui/song_list_demo.{c,h}` + a `songlist` console command (`attach`/`off`/`hide`/`show`/`solid`/`tree`/`stats`) with a catalog-backed provider; **validated on hardware**. | Matches the project's MCC-isolation idiom (own module + `user.cmake`, like `console`/`perf_log`); the widget is the primary, reusable piece, so it was built first, ahead of the MGS screen around it. Row-provider keeps the widget generic and testable and lets the screen map `Catalog_At(i)` → row (and supply tier badges once the tier column exists). Pool allocation (not static) because Legato recursively `LE_FREE`s the whole child tree on screen teardown (`legato_widget.c`) — a static widget attached to a screen would feed a static pointer to `LE_FREE`; the Legato widget pool is the accepted heap carve-out, and the widget is one self-contained allocation (fixed internal scratch, no secondary mallocs). Time-based inertia (vs per-tick) because the update cadence is render-bound (~55 Hz) and irregular under the priority-2 UI task. Tier badges blank until the deferred catalog `tier` column lands. |

| 2026-06-23 | **RTC enabled in Gregorian mode, kept in UTC; FatFs file timestamps wired to it.** Added the SAM9X75 internal RTC via MCC (Gregorian/`struct tm` plib `RTC_TimeSet/Get`, `RTC_Initialize` in `SYS_Initialize`); `RTC_MR.UTC=0` (calendar register format) but the clock *value* is set in UTC. Console gains `time` / `time set YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (prints ISO-8601 `…Z`, computes weekday). A strong `get_fattime()` in `storage.c` overrides the generated `__WEAK` stub (which hardcoded 2013-08-09) so files carry RTC time (year-1980, sec/2 DOS packing; <1980 falls back to the epoch). Resolves the timestamp half of Q12. **No backup cell populated yet** → RTC resets on power-off and must be re-set at boot (host via `time set`); persistent wall-clock needs VDDBU backup hardware. | "UTC mode" (the `RTC_MR.UTC` seconds-counter format) vs Gregorian is orthogonal to keeping a UTC *value* — Gregorian was chosen because FatFs `get_fattime` (mandatory, `FF_FS_NORTC=0`) wants broken-down fields, so calendar registers feed it directly with no epoch conversion, while a UTC value gives DST/timezone-proof timestamps matching the spec's `session_started_utc` `…Z` convention (§4.6.5). `get_fattime` as a `__WEAK` override keeps it out of the regenerated diskio.c. Player identity + whether to add VDDBU backup remain open under Q12. |
| 2026-06-23 | **SD write buffers must be cache-line aligned (`__ALIGNED(CACHE_LINE_SIZE)`, 32 B) or writes drop to ~10x-slow single-sector transfers.** First `sd bench` on hardware: read 5.7 MB/s but write **0.4 MB/s** (verify ok). Root cause is in the generated FatFs diskio (`system/fs/fat_fs/hardware_access/diskio.c`): for a buffer not aligned to `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, `disk_write` bounces through a `SYS_FS_FAT_ALIGNED_BUFFER_LEN`-sized (512 B = **one sector**) buffer in a loop → single-sector writes; `disk_read` for the same unaligned buffer still does a single multi-block transfer (reads sector 0 via the bounce buffer, the rest directly into the app buffer) → stays fast. Hence the write-slow / read-fast asymmetry with one shared buffer. Fix: aligned the bench scratch (`storage.c s_chunk`). **Confirmed on hardware: write 0.4 → 2.9 MB/s (7x), read 6.7 MB/s, verify ok.** 2.9 MB/s already clears the default recording load (§4.6.3: ~1.05 MB/s at 1 keyframe/s) with ~2.7x headroom; the §4.6.2 "≥5 MB/s" was a ceiling the format sits well under, not the workload. | The `sd bench` smoke test earned its keep on day one — caught a silent 14x write penalty before any recorder was built on top. **System-wide implication:** every buffer handed to `SYS_FS_FileWrite` for throughput (M4 keyframe DDR buffers, config/catalog/results writers) wants 32 B alignment, else it silently runs at single-sector speed. The ISC capture buffers are DMA-aligned already, but app-side staging buffers are the risk. Speed-mode-DEFAULT is *not* the bottleneck (reads prove the link); revisit High-Speed mode only if aligned writes still fall short of the §4.6.2 ≥5 MB/s target. |

Console gains one `sd <info|ls|bench|mount|unmount>` command (binding-table row + `cmd_sd`, dispatch into the storage diag API via a `storage_print_fn` adapter so storage doesn't depend on the console). `Storage_Initialize` runs in `APP_Initialize` (state only — no pre-scheduler I/O). **Code-complete; pending Greg's MPLAB build + on-hardware test.** MCC config observed: SDMMC card-detect = `DRV_SDMMC_CD_METHOD_POLLING`, `isFsEnabled=true`, speed `DRV_SDMMC_SPEED_MODE_DEFAULT`, 4-bit; SYS_FS `AUTOMOUNT=false`, `VOLUME_NUMBER=1`, `MAX_FILES=1`. | SDMMC + FatFs failures are hardware/clock/format/cache-coherence issues; a standalone smoke test isolates them from the first real consumer (M4 record task / catalog loader), which would otherwise debug mount + FatFs + its own logic at once. Console-command form (vs throwaway code) is near-free in the embedded-cli idiom and stays a permanent field diagnostic, like `t1s`/`nodes`. `sd bench` (write→read-back→verify, integer MB/s) directly validates the ≥5 MB/s the recording format assumes (spec §4.6.2) **before** building the recorder — note speed mode is DEFAULT (not High-Speed), so that's the lever to pull if the number falls short rather than reshaping the keyframe stride. Manual mount needs no further MCC regen (we just did one); the `Storage_Mount` seam is isolated so the production path swaps to an automount event handler (correct hotplug for the removable card) without touching the diagnostics. `MAX_FILES=1` is fine for sequential catalog/art loads; bump if concurrent opens are needed. |
| 2026-06-22 | **SD-card data architecture decided (spec §4.6.2 layout, §4.7, §4.8.3, new §4.8.6/§4.8.7).** The flash-vs-SD line is drawn by *what the data is coupled to*. **Flash (compile-time):** recognizer signatures (centroids, menu baselines, song bitmap templates, thresholds) **and** the menu graph — all *algorithm-coupled* (only valid with the exact grid/sampling/normalization compiled into `gameplay_classify.c`/`gameplay_select.c`), generated by `tools/gameplay/gameplay/export_c.py` into `gameplay_metadata.h`. **Recognition never reads the SD card.** **SD (FAT32, `/marvin/`):** `config.json` (resolves Q9 — config on SD, not flash); `games/<game>/songs.json` song-catalog *labels* keyed by the recognizer's `(setlist,index)` (`gp_song_t`), carrying a `metadata_version` stamp so the UI can warn on a stale catalog; `games/<game>/art/<setlist>-<NN>.{jpg,png}` album artwork (named by the same key, decoded once at startup into a static DDR cache via Legato's already-enabled JPEG/PNG decoders — no new library); `players/results.jsonl` append-only per-player results; and `recordings/`. **Rejected: moving recognition data to SD** — it's an algorithm-coupled build artifact, not user-meaningful, and a stale SD blob would silently misclassify; keeping it compile-time removes that failure mode entirely. New open Qs: Q12 (results timestamps — enable SAM9X75 RTC for wall-clock vs `session`+`frame_epoch`; player identity) and Q13 (artwork target dimensions + cache pixel format). **Doc-only change; no firmware** — all loaders/writers build on the M4 FAT mount (results also need M9 Phase 3 score readers). | This is the design Greg raised (song metadata + artwork + per-player results on SD; could recognition be data-driven from a file?). Recognition turned out to already be data-driven, but coupled to the C algorithm, so the right split is the one spec §4.8.3 always anticipated: compile-time tables for the algorithm-coupled graph/signatures, SD for the user-meaningful, independently-changing data. Keying catalog/artwork/results by the stable `(setlist,index)` the recognizer emits means a missing/stale card degrades gracefully (Unknown song / placeholder) and never breaks recognition. Legato's runtime JPEG+PNG decoders are already enabled (`legato_config.h`), so no TJpgDec; the internal RTC exists (32.768 kHz + VDDBU backup on the Hybrid board) but is currently disabled, so wall-clock timestamps are an opt-in cost (MCC regen + backup-cell), not assumed. |

The fretboard node (id 1) stays in the table as the **detector/RX** source for when it moves to T1S; only the actuator TX target moved. | The actuation role split out to the dedicated `guitar` node, which is now proven on the bus (guitar G1: `LAN8651 up … PLCA follower id=2/8`). marvin was still addressing its 1-byte command to the fretboard node; with the guitar running non-promiscuous (MAC-filtered), commands had to be re-pointed at `02:..:02` to actuate it. Single guitar today, so the target is `node_for_type(T1S_NODE_GUITAR)`; an **active-guitar selector** (mirror of `Detector_SetActive`) is deferred until multiple guitar nodes share the bus. RX/ADC over T1S is unaffected (no T1S detector node exists yet — the real fretboard is still UART). The "FretboardLink" module keeps its name for now (broader rename deferred). |
